Qualcomm is betting big on data centers as smartphone sales sink

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Qualcomm expects data center chips will eventually account for 30% of its business.

Qualcomm expects data center chips will eventually account for 30% of its business.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

With smartphone sales tanking by around 20 percent compared to 2025, Qualcomm is looking to data centers to help boost its bottom line. During its Q4 2026 earnings call, the company laid out its plans to make a splash in the AI data center business, through partnerships with companies like Amazon and Meta announced earlier this year. The company expects that portion of its business to grow quickly, forecasting around $650 million in data center revenue during the first quarter of 2027. But it anticipates that will reach $5 billion by the end of 2027 and $15 billion by 2029.

Considering the company’s total revenue for 2026 was $10.4 billion, that would constitute a significant share of its earnings. It confirmed during the call that it expects data centers to eventually account for around 30 percent of its business.

That’s not quite as ambitious as AMD, which says that it expects over 80 percent of its revenue will eventually come from data centers. But it suggests there’s plenty of demand that needs to be filled and enough AI money to go around.

Who Is Kaylub Giamanco? What to Know About Dad Who Killed Oregon School Shooter

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

A heroic father is being credited with stopping a gunman at his daughter’s Oregon elementary school after confronting the teen shooter inside the building. Kaylub Giamanco has been identified as the armed civilian who shot and killed a 16-year-old attacker on October 15, 2026, at Silver Rail Elementary School in Bend, Oregon.

Giamanco — a father of two students at the school — intervened after the shooter had already killed three children and a teacher inside a special needs classroom. Newly released 911 audio captures Giamanco’s warning to a dispatcher that he was about to take action.

Authorities released the dramatic 911 call Giamanco placed just before 1 p.m. on October 15, moments before he entered the building and stopped the shooter.

Giamanco told the dispatcher there was “someone with a gun” at his daughter’s school as he was driving home for lunch.

“I have a gun and I will shoot them right now,” Giamanco said.

The operator urged him not to get involved, but the father refused to stay back. “This is my daughter’s school!” he shouted. “I’m about to go kill this kid.”

When asked if he knew the identity of the gunman, Giamanco responded, “No I don’t f***ing know I was just driving to the school.”

He then described the shooter — who has since been identified as 16-year-old Griffin J. John — breaching the building. “They’re on the south side entering the building right now,” he said.

“Is he shooting?” the dispatcher asked. Giamanco confirmed shots had been fired. The 911 operator tried to get a more comprehensive physical description, but the call was cut off before Giamanco could answer.

Officers arrived at the school at 1:02 p.m. — four minutes after John was first spotted climbing the fence and two minutes after Giamanco placed his call. By the time police arrived, John was dead.

Giamanco opened up about the harrowing moments in an interview with Oregon Public Broadcasting.

“I just was in full panic, like, ‘No, there’s children, there’s babies in there,’” he recalled.

Giamanco said that when he spotted John climbing the school fence, “panic fled into my brain, and that’s when I opened my center console.”

“That’s when I started hearing children pleading for their lives and then I started hearing gunfire and I just started Army crawling faster and faster and I told myself this is probably the last thing that I’m ever going to do,” he told OPB.

Once inside the school, Giamanco made his way to the special education classroom, where he saw a teacher struggling with John. He fired at the shooter, allowing the teacher to escape the room.

“He was scrambling for his weapon,” Giamanco said of John. “I looked at him and I shot him two more times to make sure that he couldn’t move because I was afraid of him shooting anybody else.”

Before being stopped, John — who was armed with his mother’s shotgun — shot through locked doors and killed three children and a teacher in the special needs classroom. One other student was critically wounded and another teacher was injured.

Bend Police Chief Mike Krantz said the shooter was a previous special needs student at the school. Authorities confirmed John fired as many as 15 shots with a 12-gauge shotgun and had 44 rounds remaining at the time he was killed.

Those closest to Giamanco say his instinct to act didn’t surprise them.

“Kaylub has always been a hero from the time we were 6 years old,” Dyland Costa, a childhood friend, told NBC News. “He’s always been the type to spring into action when needed.”

Giamanco’s mother, Kenda Freeman, recounted the phone call she received from her son after the shooting.

“He said, ‘Mom, I just shot and killed somebody.’ And I said, ‘Kaylub, tell me about it.’ And he said, ‘A man went into my daughters’ school and, um, shot kids.’ And he said, ‘And I looked at him point blank and I shot and killed him, mom,’” Freeman told CBS LA. “I told him he saved a lot of lives and he said, ‘Mom, I couldn’t save them all.’”

Freeman went on to describe how her son intervened that day. “Kaylub has a genuine heart — he loves his community. Kaylub knew something was not right, so he pulled up onto the grass, grabbed his gun and basically stalked the gunman back into the school. He crawled through a window that the gunman had shot out — he Army-crawled through the window.”

Freeman said the aftermath has been difficult for her son, who is “having a hard time with it — again, you know, the idea of not being able to save them all, right? So when he closes his eyes the things he thinks about [are] what could I have done differently? How could I have done it better?”

She added that Giamanco is “not seeking recognition” for his actions but rather “wishes to express his gratitude to the first responders, school personnel and community for the outpouring of love and support.”

While Freeman said she is “thankful that my babies are OK,” she acknowledged that many other families were devastated.

“That’s hard to swallow, because my heart goes out to those families who didn’t get to bring their babies home,” she said.

Rhoam Summer, 5, was identified as the youngest of the victims by his family, per NBC News.

“We are broken and lost, but are surrounded by friends and family who are keeping us safe,” Rhoam’s mother, Kelik Summer, wrote on Facebook after the shooting. Officials have declined to publicly identify the remaining child victims—an 8-year-old and an 11-year-old—at the request of their families.

Megan Lambert, a 25-year-old teacher, was also killed in the attack. A 7-year-old girl and another teacher, Becky Drury, were injured in the attack.

The shooter’s mother, Shauntai Nickerson, expressed grief in the aftermath of the shooting. “I miss my boy,” she said of her son, per multiple media outlets including The New York Times and CNN. “I’m so sorry, I wish I could have done something. I’m sorry for their loss.”

How to get Apex Protocol Gear set in The Division 2

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The Apex Protocol Gear set is a new addition to The Division 2, and it’s tied directly to the Echoes of Central Park DLC. The DLC has added a new playable area, and you get new bosses to kill.

These bosses are directly involved in providing you with the equipment pieces, but the job is easier said than done.

For starters, you will need to have access to the Echoes of Central Park DLC. Without it, you will not have access to the new location, which is mandatory for farming this gear set. As of writing, there is no other possible way to get the Apex Protocol gear pieces aside from playing in Central Park.

Once you have access to the location, your next task is to finish the main story campaign. It’s a relatively easy one, and you can also finish it as a beginner. Veterans can bring their Prototype gear, but the bonus effects will be unavailable at this location.

When you finish the campaign, you will learn about the Guardians. They were all former SHD agents who got involved in nefarious activities. This completely metamorphosed them and turned them into the apex predators. To get your gear pieces, you will need to defeat all of them.

There are five of them located all around the map, and you can see their locations on the image above. Head to the marked spot, and you will find the Lair of a Guardian. Once you reach a lair, you will have to deal enough damage to make the Guardian appear.

You will then have a set amount of time to kill the Guardian. As you deal damage, you will have the possibility of receiving the gear pieces as reward loot. The odds are not listed by Ubisoft, but they certainly go up at higher difficulties in my testing.

Hence, playing at Heroic will be the most rewarding thing if you do not mind the challenge and simply want to increase your luck of finding these pieces of gear.

JD Vance Turns on Trump With 'Obvious' Reason for GOP's Midterm Losses

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Vice President JD Vance is seemingly attempting to distance himself from President Donald Trump after the Republicans’ massive 2026 midterm election losses.

“One of the things the data analysts on our side obsessed over was ‘composition’ — whether the electorate looked close enough to 2024 for Republicans to win,” Vance, 42, wrote via X on Wednesday, November 4. “Despite polls that showed a Democrat blowout, the data kept on telling us the same story: in some states turnout was merely OK, in other states it was downright amazing, and in no state was it catastrophic.”

He continued, “There was a genuine sense of optimism on our team: our people were showing up. So, I asked the smartest data guy I know, does that mean we’re going to shock the world? ‘No,’ he told me. ‘We might do better than the polls suggest. But the data is telling us that a lot of Trump/Vance 2024 voters are pissed and planning to vote for the other guys. Our voters are turning out, but they may not vote for us.’”

After the Tuesday, November 3, elections, the Democrats thwarted the GOP, taking control of both the House of Representatives and the Senate while turning several traditionally “red” states “blue,” including Texas.

Vance claims that despite the unnamed pollster speculating that Republican voters wouldn’t show up, GOP voters made their way to the polls on election day — they just didn’t vote for Republican candidates.

“Our voters did show up. In Florida, for example, the electorate was 9 points more Republican than Democrat, just a couple points worse than 2024,” he wrote on Wednesday. “Undoubtedly there were places we could have done better on turnout. But the main problem is not turnout. The main problem is that many people who voted for Trump/Vance in 2024 voted for Democrats in 2026. There will be a lot of infighting and arguing over what happened and why that is.”

Vance turned to another pollster, who found Trump, 80, and Vance’s economic viewpoint “wasn’t sharp enough down the stretch.”

“I’m sure that’s true, but the real reason we lost is obvious to almost everyone,” Vance noted. “Another pollster … saw that a lot of Trump 2024 voters were angry and planning to vote for Democrats. He asked those voters who had abandoned us what would cause them to come back on our team.”

According to the strategist, voters wanted Trump to “end the war” in Iran, focus on inflation, put America first and change the country’s relationship with Israel. (Trump initiated the conflict in Iran in February, coordinating joint airstrikes with Israel that subsequently shut down the Strait of Hormuz, increasing the cost of gas, diesel and several daily goods.)

“In the next few months, there’s going to be a lot of gaslighting. Expect to hear any number of establishment politicians say the solution to our woes is amnesty,” Vance stated. “I’m open to the idea that we should change enforcement — by focusing more on businesses that hire illegal labor, for instance. But anyone pushing amnesty is lying to you.”

He continued, “The reason we lost is the war. The president himself said so last night. If you’d like to argue it was worth it, that losing the Senate for the next 6 years was worth it, then do so. Argue it was a worthwhile cost to pay. Agree or disagree, that’s at least honest.”

Vance further acknowledged that “everyone knows why” the GOP lost the midterms.

“I remain hopeful about our country. To the people who are pissed off and want to say, ‘I told you so:’ you’re right, you did tell us so,” he concluded. “Take that energy into electing Republicans who share your worldview. To the people who are just pissed at the results, you should be. Let’s take that anger and get back to the work of saving our country.”

Scott McTominay on target again in Champions League as Scotland duty beckons for ex-Man Utd man

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Scott McTominay was on target again in the Champions League on Wednesday night in his penultimate match for Napoli before joining up with the Scotland squad.

The 29-year-old netted his fifth goal in seven starts since returning from a minor operation to correct an irregular heart rhythm but ended up on the losing side as Napoli crashed to a 4-1 defeat in Porto.

Did you know with a Digital Subscription to The Scotsman, you can get unlimited access to the website including our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

McTominay struck in the 43rd minute to level the match at 1-1, pouncing on a rebound to fire home a first-time volley from eight yards after former Manchester United teammate Rasmus Hojlund saw an effort saved.

The goal cancelled out Jan Bednarek's opener for Porto but the hosts went back in front through Gabri Veiga in the 71st minute before Jakob Kiwior made it 3-1 with 10 minutes remaining.

Napoli were then reduced to 10 men in the 88th minute when Rafa Marín was shown a red card for denying a goalscoring opportunity, with Samu Aghehowa converting the resultant penalty.

Billy Gilmour completed the full 90 minutes for Napoli - the first time the Scotland midfielder has started and finished a match for the Italian club since September.

McTominay, meanwhile, has now scored three goals in his last two Champions League appearances after netting a brace in a 4-3 win over Bodo/Glimt at Stadio Diego Armando Maradona last month. He followed that with another double in a 3-3 draw with Roma in Serie A.

Scotland head coach Sebastien Pocognoli will hope the talismanic midfielder can bring his goalscoring form to the international scene ahead of the upcoming Nations League matches against North Macedonia and Switzerland.

McTominay missed the Belgian's maiden camp last month due to the minimally invasive medical procedure which also saw him sit out four Napoli matches.

His goal against Porto takes McTominay's overall Champions League tally to eight, making him Scotland's leading scorer in the competition since it rebranded from the European Cup in 1993, three ahead of Kenny Miller and Ian Durrant on five.

Napoli host Lazio on Sunday in their final Serie A match before the international break, with McTominay and Gilmour due to meet with up the Scotland squad the following day.

Mass Effect Fans Get Enough Signatures To Plead To The Canadian Government For BioWare’s Life

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

In the two weeks since the closure of BioWare,Â fans of the Canadian RPG studio have been rallying around different petitions and causes, whether they be advocating for the revival of the studio’s canceledÂ Mass Effect: Universe or asking publisher Electronic Arts to give the IP to a new studio that could make games in its sci-fi universe instead. However, following calls from Canadian politician Avi Lewis for the government to help assist BioWare workers in buying the studio and going independent, fans have put together a petition to bring the notion to the Canadian House of Commons, and they’ve already received enough signatures to do so.

The petition, sponsored by Parliament member Don Davies, argues that BioWare is one of Canada’s “most important cultural exports” and that the shuttering by American parent company Electronic Arts “represents an immense loss to and possibly a targeted attack on Canada’s cultural legacy.”

We, the undersigned, citizens and residents of Canada, call upon the Government of Canada to:

1. Provide BioWare with the capital necessary to secure independence of themselves and their intellectual property from Electronic Arts and resume business as an independent, Canadian company;

2. Protect Canadian businesses and cultural institutions from being dictated by American and foreign corporate interests; and

3. Champion the values of inclusivity, tolerance, democracy, justice, and civil rights at the centre of Canada’s national identity by preserving BioWare’s invaluable contribution to the global entertainment industry.

As laid out on the House of Commons website, petitions like these require 500 validated signatures from Canadian citizens, presumably so they can’t be spammed by people outside of the country who support a cause, and it has surpassed that number. This means that it will be presented or tabled in the House of Commons, so there will be some kind of official response to this petition.

It’s worth noting that, regardless of what the Canadian government decides to do here, EA still owns BioWare and its IP, so it would require the company’s cooperation and approval. EA historically doesn’t sell its IP or studios, which many assume is why it didn’t sell off BioWare following its acquisition by private equity earlier this year, so don’t get your hopes up just yet.

Reports that followed BioWare’s closure claim that EA decided to shutter the studio and cancel Mass Effect: UniverseÂ after weighing its budget and choosing to prioritizeÂ Star Wars: Battlefront 3, a game that it reportedly hasn’t begun actual development on, over BioWare’s sci-fi RPG sequel.

We Tried Every Boxed Stuffing We Could Find—These Are The Only Ones We’d Buy Again

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Some mixes taste so homemade, they just might fool your Thanksgiving guests.

In my opinion, stuffing is the crème de la crème of a Thanksgiving feast. I’d choose it over any other carb or veggie side. Everyone knows stuffing balances a buttery, crispy top with a moist, tender interior and subtle aromatics. It should be the greatest complement to a turkey. But sometimes we can’t get around to making a homemade stuffing on Thanksgiving, so we settle for a box mix.

We tried a variety of stuffings so you don’t have to wonder which one to pick up at the grocery store. We were looking for stuffing that could be mistaken for homemade, with a balanced texture and real seasoning. We tried eight stuffings, but only five were worthy of adding to your holiday table or weeknight rotation.

This ShopRite stuffing is super weeknight-friendly, and I wouldn’t hesitate to make this for an easy dinner side. Because it’s made with chicken broth, you can use water to make it without losing too much flavor.

This Pepperidge Farm Stuffing was the most surprising one I tried. Because it contains whole wheat and therefore is a little darker in color, I wasn’t sure I would like it. The flavor was excellent, though, and I really appreciated the homemade feel of having to add your own fresh chopped onion and celery.

It's admittedly been years since I had my end-all, be-all favorite boxed stuffing as a kid, Stovetop’s Savory Herbs. And you know what? It really holds up.

As the name suggests, this boxed stuffing mix is Big-S Savory and loaded with herbs, with a buttery, peppery backbone. It gets perfectly crispy around the edges. It’s both nostalgic-good and delicious enough to keep a box on hand for after Turkey Day, if not just to make these sausage stuffing bites.

Here is where the fooling comes in, with Kinder's Brown Butter & Herbs Stuffing Mix. One bite of this garlicky, nutty stuffing had me say out loud, “Oh, this is deluxe.”

Not only that, but this might be the easiest stuffing of them all, with no fewer than three different ways to prepare and serve it. There’s a sausage-spiked version, a skillet-made version, and an ultra-savory version with leeks. In short, if you're really in the mood for a homemade stuffing experience, but you're running out of prep time, Kinder's stuffing will trick everyone into thinking you made this fully on your own.

While Kinder’s is great when you still want to eat like a Thanksgiving maximalist, some of us want the easiest (but no less delicious) route. In which case, Mrs. Cubbison’s Traditional Stuffing will take you there.

I’ve long been a fan of the brand’s herby, buttery croutons, so I’m not surprised that Mrs. Cubbison’s Traditional Stuffing is yet another one of its products I'll always keep on hand. The bread base is just the right amount of toasted and caramelized, making for a stuffing that's tender and crispy around the edges (no mush here). Plus, it'll even free up some space in your oven since it can be made in the slow cooker, too.

Qantas Reaches Deal to Sell 44 Boeing 737s

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Qantas has agreed to sell 44 Boeing 737-800s to British aviation aftermarket specialist AerFin as the Australian carrier prepares to retire most of its 737 fleet.

The deal includes the aircraft’s 88 CFM56-7B engines. AerFin said Wednesday it expects to receive the first jet before the end of 2026, with the rest transferring over the next seven years as Qantas retires them.

Qantas Domestic CEO Markus Svensson said the agreement gives the airline “flexibility to adjust the timing as new aircraft arrive,” in a news release.

Qantas operates nearly 80 Boeing 737-800s, according to data from planespotters.net. The carrier ordered 28 Airbus A321XLRs in 2022 to begin replacing the Boeing jets and later ordered another 20. Seven A321XLRs have joined the fleet, with 41 still on order.

AerFin, which supplies parts and engines to airlines and maintenance providers, said the acquisition is the largest in its history. The company said the deal would help it supply aircraft and engine parts to customers worldwide.

Democrats flip dozens of GOP seats. Republicans haven't flipped a single one

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Seven Senate seats. At least 24 House districts. Five governorships. Twelve state legislative chambers. By Wednesday afternoon, that's how far Democrats' historic midterm rout had reached.

Republicans, meanwhile, had yet to flip a single Democratic-held congressional seat, governorship or state legislative chamber, according to Associated Press data. Their remaining opportunities for congressional pickups are largely confined to two newly gerrymandered House districts that weren't even supposed to be competitive in the first place.

The lopsided results underscored the breadth of the GOP's shellacking, with Democrats breaking decades of Republican dominance in reliably red states and reshaping the balance of power from Washington to state capitols.

As of late Wednesday, nine House seats remained uncalled by the AP along with two Senate seats and two governors races. The one seat Republicans may have a chance of flipping that hasn't been redistricted ahead of this election is Maine's 2nd District, FiftyPlusOne head of research Mary Radcliffe said by email.

In Kansas, voters elected their first Democratic U.S. senator since 1932, ending a 94-year losing streak for the party. In Texas, Democrats toppled three-term Republican Gov. Greg Abbott and captured a Senate seat that had eluded the party since 1988. Republican-held Senate seats also fell in Iowa, South Carolina and Ohio, along with seats in Maine and North Carolina.

The same wave swept through governors' mansions. Democrats scored upsets in Georgia, Iowa, Ohio and Nevada — all states President Donald Trump won in 2024 — giving the party a majority of the nation's governorships for the first time since 2010.

Upheaval even reached state capitols, where Republican legislative majorities that had endured for decades fell like dominoes.

Democrats seized control of the Texas House for the first time in more than two decades, flipped both chambers of the Wisconsin Legislature and wrested the North Carolina House from Republicans after 16 years in the minority.

In Pennsylvania, Democrats captured the state Senate for the first time since before 1994, giving the party complete control of state government. They also reclaimed lower chambers in Michigan and Minnesota, restoring full Democratic control in both states. In Arizona, unofficial results pointed toward Democrats taking both legislative chambers for the first time in roughly 60 years.

Even where Republicans held on to legislative majorities, Democrats stripped away veto-proof supermajorities in Florida, Indiana, Kansas and Missouri. Not since the aftermath of Watergate in 1974 had the GOP faced a statehouse reckoning of this magnitude, according to the Democratic Legislative Campaign Committee.

The scale of the losses is all the more remarkable given how aggressively Republicans had tried to protect their power. At Trump's urging, GOP lawmakers spent months pursuing an extraordinary mid-decade redistricting campaign, redrawing congressional boundaries in Texas, Florida and North Carolina to tilt the electoral map in their favor.

Yet the gerrymandering push did little to blunt the Democratic wave. And the damage might not be over. Republicans were still fighting to hold Senate seats in Alaska and Mississippi, with several House contests also yet to be called Wednesday.

— Kevin Breuninger contributed to this report.

A genuinely outsized move for Bitcoin (BTC) may still be on the table before January 1, 2027, as Mark Zuckerberg’s Meta AI predicts an unconventional but not impossible catalyst could skyrocket BTC USD price to incredible new heights by the start of next year.

The hypothetical scenario being considered involves a coordinated effort by several of the world’s largest energy producers and commodity trading companies to settle a significant portion of cross-border trades in oil, gas, and refined products using Bitcoin.

In this scenario, Bitcoin would transition from being solely a store-of-value asset to becoming a preferred method for settlement and liquidity in physical energy markets. This shift could create a new, high-velocity demand channel not seen in previous market cycles.

This Meta AI prediction drops as Bitcoin trades around $77,000, rebounding +1.8% overnight after a hefty selloff that pushed it to $75,800. Bitcoin is currently trading in a broad consolidation range after the recent pullback. The market has been grinding between roughly the mid-$70,000s and the low-$80,000s, with sellers still active near recent local highs and buyers defending the lower end of the range.

On the weekly chart, the structure remains intact as long as price holds above the cluster of supports built earlier in the year. A decisive weekly close above the $90,000–$95,000 zone would mark a clear shift in intermediate momentum and open the door to a more aggressive revaluation.

In a high-conviction breakout environment, measured moves from the current base and extensions of prior impulse legs would project substantially higher targets than most participants currently model.

One of the most regrettable casualties of EA's decision to shut down treasured RPG maker BioWare – well bellow the dozens of people who lost their jobs – was Mass Effect 5, the long-awaited sequel that was revealed in 2020. The project was canceled alongside EA's latest round of layoffs, but a coordinated social media effort to rescue it has attracted the attention and support of a BioWare veteran, studio co-founder Trent Oster.

I love seeing all the energy around #SaveMassEffect I think there is a still a great thesis for a well-funded RPG developer who can deliver. Makes me want to get some of the original band back together.November 4, 2026

That said, the organizers behind the #SaveMassEffect movement have started a petition on Change.org that's about to cross 300,000 signatures, and a separate petition from the same group seeking acknowledgment from the Canadian government just today reached a major milestone.

Whether Mass Effect 5 comes back from the dead or not, Oster isn't taking EA's shutdown of evidence that big-budget RPGs are going the way of the dodo. Quite the opposite, actually, as he seemingly agrees with a commenter arguing that there's an "RPG resurgence" happening right now. "Seeing BG3 sales numbers may have had an effect," Oster says about the current state of RPG development.

Swift has fallen: NASA's 22-year-old space telescope burns up over Sumatra

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The reentry of NASA's dead Swift space telescope is the end of an era for rapid-response space science.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

It's the end of an era: NASA's Swift telescope has reached the conclusion of its nearly 22-year mission, burning up in Earth's atmosphere after studying some of the universe's most powerful and mysterious events.

According to NASA, the spacecraft reentered the atmosphere at "7:39 a.m. EST Wednesday over the North Sumatra region of Indonesia, at approximately 3.4 degrees north latitude and 98.6 degrees east longitude." NASA expected most of the telescope's structure to burn up during its passage through Earth's atmosphere, estimating a 1-in-2,100 chance of any surviving debris falling over a populated area. No damage or injuries have been reported.

Weighing approximately 3,200 pounds (1,450 kilograms), the Neil Gehrels Swift Observatory was initially designed for a two-year mission, but it far exceeded expectations. Over two decades, Swift became an important tool in the study of gamma-ray bursts, supernovas, black holes and other cosmic phenomena.

Launched in November 2004, Swift was designed to detect gamma-ray bursts, powerful explosions that occur throughout the universe. Its three onboard telescopes let scientists observe cosmic events across different wavelengths, including ultraviolet, optical and X-ray light. The telescope could swivel to focus on different sky targets in a matter of minutes, allowing it to rapidly follow up on sudden events like supernova explosions.

NASA estimates that Swift's scientific observations have contributed to approximately 7,000 research papers, leaving behind an extensive collection that scientists can continue studying for years.

Swift's return was driven by atmospheric drag, which eventually slowed the spacecraft and caused its orbit to decay. Increased solar activity intensified the drag, thus accelerating the space telescope's descent toward Earth.

Earlier this year, NASA explored an ambitious rescue mission to extend Swift's operational life. In August 2026, a privately developed robotic spacecraft built by Katalyst Space Technologies was intended to rendezvous with Swift and boost it into a higher orbit. However, the mission failed after launching, leaving the observatory on its path toward reentry.

Get the world’s most fascinating discoveries delivered straight to your inbox.

NASA officially ended Swift's scientific operations on Oct. 20 after its declining altitude made observations too difficult. For NASA and the broader astronomy community, Swift's story is a reminder that even missions designed to last just a few years can fundamentally change our understanding of our universe.

Kenna Hughes-Castleberry is the Content Manager at Live Science. Formerly, she was the Content Manager at Space.com and before that the Science Communicator at JILA, a physics research institute. Kenna is also a book author, with her upcoming book 'Octopus X' scheduled for release in spring of 2027. Her beats include physics, health, environmental science, technology, AI, animal intelligence, corvids, and cephalopods.

BitMart founder says security team ‘severely understaffed’ after new hack

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The crypto exchange’s founder warned of security staffing shortages amid restructuring as analysts flagged suspicious transfers potentially linked to the Mercado Bitcoin hack.

Cryptocurrency exchange BitMart is operating with an understaffed security team following restructuring, its founder said Wednesday, after confirming a new hack that may be linked to the recent attack on Brazil’s exchange Mercado Bitcoin.

In a post on X, BitMart founder Sheldon Xia said threats against employees and alleged retaliation by former staff had complicated efforts to retain technical personnel.

“It took considerable effort and individual conversations to persuade some key technical staff to remain and maintain our core systems,” Xia wrote. “However, we continue to face numerous attacks. Our security team is currently severely understaffed, but we are still doing everything we can to safeguard data and assets.”

Xia described the amount involved in Monday’s hack as small but did not disclose a figure. Blockchain analytics firm Bitquery identified approximately $391,000 in suspicious transfers from wallets mostly associated with BitMart across three blockchains, according to a Wednesday investigation.

Bitquery traced the funds to a wallet also implicated in the more than $46 million Mercado Bitcoin hack on Monday, Nov. 2, supporting Xia’s preliminary suspicion of a possible connection between the incidents.

Related: North Korea denies role in $6.3B Binance hack, points finger at US

The latest breach comes amid months of uncertainty over BitMart’s future. The exchange announced in July that it would end trading on Aug. 26 and cease operations in January 2027. Following the announcement, users reported delayed withdrawals and frozen accounts, raising concerns about access to their funds.

In August, BitMart’s Chinese-language X account alleged that employees had gone unpaid and demanded answers about customer funds. The founder dismissed the allegations as fabricated, though it remained unclear who controlled the account at the time.

In a restructuring proposal published Monday, the exchange outlined three potential recovery options for users, including liquid asset distributions and recovery-linked tokens, with plans to seek court approval in December or January.

Cointelegraph contacted BitMart for details about the restructuring, security staffing and latest breach but had not received a response by publication.

Separately, a crypto crime-fighting alliance formed by Tron, Tether and TRM Labs has dissolved amid a wave of major industry hacks, Bloomberg reported Wednesday.

The T3 Financial Crime Unit, launched in September 2024 and later joined by Binance, struggled with slow responses and poor coordination, Bloomberg reported, citing people familiar with the matter. One source said the group disbanded in late August.

In May, T3 said it had helped freeze more than $450 million in assets linked to suspected criminal activity since its launch in 2024.

The group’s dissolution comes amid a wave of major crypto hacks, including last week’s $6.3 billion Binance breach, the largest cryptocurrency theft on record.

For those who believe in him, God is pure spirit, beyond all gender. So, technically, God is nonbinary. But, anyway, Jesus never said fck all about transgender people.

For his part, Adam Hamilton is the founder of a huge Methodist church in the Kansas City area. He has given sermons in which he asked his congregation to repent of eating beef and driving cars as part of a sermon on climate change. The Republicans attempted to freakify him with these ideas, but Hamilton’s fundamental decency, and his sincere belief in “stewardship” of the planet, which used to be the Republican dodge on environmental issues, were able to overcome the usual campaign slanging. His acceptance speech called for a gentler, more charitable politics. Again, a more Christian Christianity than the one purportedly practiced by the ratfcking Pharisees of the American Right.

This is a very interesting development if it survives this election cycle and if it isn’t dependent on uniquely charismatic candidates like Talarico. If the Democrats can reckon with a lost source of political power, then that cannot be a bad development.

Nvidia stock tokens show price spreads, but slippage crushes the arbitrage

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Two $1,000 comparisons leave about 1 basis point and negative 15 before fees, while access and share adjustments complicate the trade.

Tokens tracking Nvidia shares can trade at different prices across issuers and exchanges, but buying the cheaper version and selling a dearer one leaves little room for profit in two modeled comparisons published by data provider Coin Metrics on Nov. 3.

The data provider's execution study shows about one basis point remaining in one $1,000 comparison after entry and exit slippage, and a negative 15 basis points in another.

The results show how a visible price difference can shrink when an order reaches the market, while preserving a small positive remainder in the first example.

A holder must compare the same share exposure, have something to sell on the expensive side, and qualify for the route used to settle the trade.

Coin Metrics' spread and slippage chart compares buying Hyperliquid NVDAx with selling Binance NVDAb, printing a gross gap of 10 basis points, entry and exit slippage of 10 basis points, and a one-basis-point remainder before trading fees.

Its second comparison buys Hyperliquid NVDAx and sells Kraken NVDAx. There, an 18-basis-point gross gap faces 33 basis points of entry and exit slippage, leaving a negative 15-basis-point gap before fees.

Two $1,000 modeled comparisons. June–Nov. 2, 2026 data; published Nov. 3. Trading fees are excluded.

One comparison retains only 1 basis point before fees; the other is already negative.

Historical modeled results, before trading fees, not live quotes or realized returns. Labels are rounded independently; +10 and −10 do not sum to the printed +1. Exact contract mapping and all-in execution conditions are unverified.

Method: Replot literal publisher labels on matching −20 to +20 basis-point axes. Arrows connect each reported gross gap to its reported before-fee remainder, not a time series. Printed slippage and results are rounded independently; no raw data or inferred bar heights are used.

Tokenized-equity framework xStocks announced NVDAx/USDC spot trading on HyperCore on Aug. 10, separately from equity perpetuals. Moving those spot tokens to or from HyperEVM involves wrapping and bridging.

The chart's venue labels alone do not establish that transfer route for every observation in its June-November sample.

A second dataset shows why even a clean-looking price comparison needs adjustment. In a Nov. 1 correction, Bitquery normalized its Nvidia tokens per underlying share and adjusted dividend treatment.

Across its Sept. 15-Oct. 13 public-pool sample, pairwise median daily gaps among the four busiest versions rounded to no more than 0.12%. The widest pair was 0.1225%, rounded to 0.12%.

Kraken's multiplier explanation shows that dividend reinvestment can increase the share exposure displayed in an xStock balance while the number of on-chain tokens stays unchanged. For wrapped xStocks, the issuer separately says token prices must be adjusted using its multiplier.

Bitquery says their Nvidia tokens have separate contracts without a direct bridge or issuer swap between them, so buying the cheaper product does not supply the different token needed for the sale.

Kraken's Oct. 30 FAQ lists a 10-basis-point taker fee and a two-basis-point maker rebate for its Pro spot order book. App purchases using US dollars or USDG have no trading fee but may include a spread, while xStock conversions carry a 1% fee.

The issuer's primary-market documentation requires onboarding, identity and anti-money-laundering checks, and whitelisted wallets. Its named cash Market Flow lists a $5,000 minimum, 24/5 availability, and fees currently set to zero.

Kraken also excludes US persons and customers in the US, UK, Canada, and Australia from xStocks access. Compatible-wallet withdrawals are supported, but transfers into a traditional brokerage account are not. The issuer classifies xStocks as tracker certificates providing economic exposure, without direct share ownership or voting rights.

Man City are not a team in Enzo Maresca's ideal image yet - their imperfections are clear as win at Leipzig shows they're learning on the job, writes JACK GAUGHAN

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Published: 17:30 EDT, 4 November 2026 | Updated: 18:16 EDT, 4 November 2026

Watching Manchester City is not exactly straightforward at the moment. Downright confusing most of the time. Different tweaks on the same tactical theme each week, City learning on the job under new management.

Enzo Maresca recently revealed that he would rather win games 1-0 than these helter-skelter, drama-filled, error-strewn goal fests but they are not yet capable of producing that. And the players know it.

On leading in Saxony, secured by Castello Lukeba's own goal, Maresca called for Phil Foden and Abdukodir Khusanov to sprint for the bench to receive detailed instructions. As they did, Marc Guehi and Enzo Fernandez engaged in deep conversation. Gesturing arms as Erling Haaland and Elliot Anderson spoke while walking back to halfway.

The body language was as if they had gone behind. Strangely, that might encourage the boss. They know this all can improve drastically in a quest to become more cohesive. There is definitely the talent among the squad to possess a certainty of how to emphatically dominate games like this one.

None of this is particularly different to when they went on the seven-game winning run to fly out of the blocks in the Premier League, imperfections making them eminently watchable but not a team in Maresca's ideal image yet. Not by a long shot.

Later, when Anderson and Rayan Cherki were locked in debate about how to press from a goal kick, it served as a reminder of Maresca's words that this is a fresh project and change takes time. Chinks exist.

Erling Haaland came to Manchester City's rescue once more with the winning goal at Leipzig

He slid home City's winner with minutes remaining after fine work from Elliot Anderson (left)

Yet on the surface this presentable a creditable result, won by Haaland with 12 minutes left as they nestle inside the Champions League's top eight. All good for now, although added confusion comes in how little this could all mean amid an appeal of the independent commission's findings. Nobody is dissecting Maresca's evolving masterplan if they lose in court.

'We need to step up,' said Haaland, captain for the night. 'Keeping the ball, knowing when to keep it and when to go, working together offensively and defensively.'

Maresca himself admitted to shaking up the midfield and back four because he is feeling out a best XI, one he can rely upon and one that mixes that quintessentially City dominance and a more direct attacking line that can punish in a nanosecond.

'I need to understand more things about the players,' he said. 'That's why I'm so confident because I'm sure that we're going to be better and better.'

Coached by one of the Italian's closest friends and former City centre-half Martin Demichelis, Leipzig felt they deserved more. They went behind in unfortunate circumstances, although Enzo Fernandez lurked at the back post to do the honours from Nico O'Reilly's drilled cross had Lukeba not completed the job for him.

And Gianluigi Donnarumma was offering encouragement, almost embarrassed by Antonio Nusa's shot squirting through his fingers. Among other opportunities, Christoph Baumgartner curled wide from the box's edge. Baumgartner had oceans to himself amid a vacant City midfield. Anderson had gone to put out a fire out wide, vacating his holding position.

Anderson was operating there alone on the night, not something he has done regularly since a £116million summer switch, and it presented yet another iteration of Maresca's City as he attempts to locate the perfect formula.

Then two minutes summing up Donnarumma. Full stretch to acrobatically tip another Baumgartner shot wide seconds after the restart. And yet, shortly after, suffering one of those moments that are becoming far too common.

In an entertaining game, City took the lead initially when Castello Lukeba scored an own goal

Ridle Baku (right) equalised for Leipzig just after half-time due to a Gianluigi Donnarumma error

Enzo Fernandez restored City's lead with this emphatic header after Iliman Ndiaye's cross

Antonio Nusa saw Leipzig equalise for a second time before Haaland decided the game late on

RB Leipzig (4-2-3-1): Vandevoordt 6; Baku 7 (Henrichs 74, 6), Orban 6, Lukeba 4, Raum 6; El Aynaoui 6 (Seiwald 74, 6), Baumgartner 7 (Nkunku 57, 6), Banzuzi 6; Gruda 7, Romulo 6 (Gomis 67, 6), Nusa 7 (Reitz 73, 6)

Man City (4-1-4-1): Donnarumma 5; Nunes 6 (Dias 79), Khusanov 6, Guehi 6, O'Reilly 6; Anderson 8 (Bouaddi 83); Foden 6 (Doku 64, 6), Fernandez 6 (Kovacic 83), Cherki 7, Ndiaye 7 (Allan 83); Haaland 7

Ridle Baku was the beneficiary of the goalkeeper's poor positioning, forgetting about his near post, and flashed in. Somebody of Donnarumma's standing cannot allow that to happen. He would soon fumble another Baku shot, which led to a penalty appeal.

'It is not a concern but it is a mistake,' Maresca said. 'Before that he did a save that no other keeper in the world makes. Gigio knows he was not good with the goal. He's going to do better.'

City missed plenty of chances themselves before Fernandez headed in a second from Iliman Ndiaye's whipped cross but that lead only lasted three minutes, Nusa slamming into the far corner just after the hour. 'Silly goals,' Maresca said.

City kept ploughing forward, Anderson kept driving on. Ndiaye set Anderson storming down the right and he knew the area to hit. Back post, Haaland. A wicked cross, a striker's finish. Game won. Maybe that's what they were chatting about earlier.

Final Fantasy 10 Blitzball Was Inspired By Star Wars Podracing

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Final Fantasy has a lot of minigames but few are as bizarre and as beloved as Blitzball, a version of underwater soccer invented for Final Fantasy X. That game’s director and producer, Square Enix veteran Yoshinori Kitase, recently revealed one of the inspirations for the fictional sport: Star Wars.

Blitzball is played in a giant sphere of water that hovers in the air. It’s like a combination of soccer and rugby, with players ramming into each other and doing special kick moves to try to score points by knocking the ball into a high-tech backboard.

Unlike most Final Fantasy minigames, Blitzball plays a major role in FFX‘s story. In addition to being the backdrop for the game’s stunning FMV cutscene intro, it’s also the main vocation of the central protagonist, Titus. NPCs you meet out in the world can be recruited to play on your team, and finishing at the top of the league is required to unlock a special late-game item.

So how did Kitase come up with it? He was talking about how Clair Obscur: Expedition 33 took inspiration from Final Fantasy when he mentioned that his own work on the franchise was inspired in part by things like Star Wars.

“One more [example of a Star Wars influence in FF] that came to my mind is from Final Fantasy X, where I had to create [Blitzball] for the game inside the fantasy setting,” he told IGN at the Lucca Comics & Games Festival 2026. “And I used an idea from Star Wars Episode I where there’s this [pod]race and the fact of using sport inside a fantasy context, I took this idea directly from Star Wars.”

Star Wars creator George Lucas’ own idea for podracing grew out of his love of car racing and hot rod culture. Little did he know it would help spawn a completely different fictional sport in a completely different universe. Weirdly enough, Blitzball has been mentioned in the Star Wars galaxy before.

Author Mike Chen namedropped it in his Star Wars novel Brotherhood. Confirming that it was a deliberate reference to Final Fantasy and not just a name he came up with independently, back in 2022 he tweeted, “Blitzball is basically underwater hockey played as a minigame in Final Fantasy X. As a former NHL writer, I freaking loved Blitzball and spent hours winning leagues.”

7 solo travel mistakes to avoid on your first trip

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

If you have yet to take a solo trip, there are many reasons why you really should. Solo travel helps you build confidence and new skills. It also provides time to reflect, recharge and reconnect with yourself — so you can return home the best version of yourself for the people who rely on you day-to-day.

I know it can be daunting to embark on your first big trip alone. But never fear, intrepid soloist. I've rounded up the top solo travel mistakes so that you can avoid them to ensure your trip goes smoothly.

Related: Travel mistakes that could ruin your next trip

One of the perks of solo travel is that you get to decide where to stay and what to do, so put some thought into it. This will ensure you have the best trip possible.

A good starting point is to check the State Department travel advisory for your destination, which will help you understand any safety concerns and identify neighborhoods you may want to avoid. It's also a good idea to enroll your trip in STEP, so you'll be alerted to things like natural disasters and security threats.

Safety isn't the only thing to consider. Are you looking for a quiet, relaxing getaway or a bustling neighborhood with endless things to do? Would you rather stay close to the action or save money by staying in a less popular area? Choose your accommodations accordingly.

Don't make the mistake I made on my first solo trip and not plan any activities. Spontaneity sounded fun, but I found making decisions on my own much harder than expected. You don't need to have your entire itinerary planned out to the minute, but doing some planning before you travel will reduce your stress during the trip.

Plus, many popular tourist attractions and trendy restaurants fill up quickly — and waiting in long lines is even less fun with no one to talk to. Do your research, and if needed, make a reservation or book a timed-entry ticket ahead of time.

Sometimes, the difference between traveling solo and with others really shines through in the little things...like using an airport bathroom.

You can't simply ask a companion to hold your luggage while you run to the restroom or figure out how to operate a metro ticket machine; you must keep track of it at all times. So think twice about packing that extra-large checked bag or hauling a not-so-ergonomic shoulder duffel on an international trip, as you will have to take it with you literally everywhere.

I personally prefer a backpack and small rolling carryon, which I can easily maneuver through crowded airports and over cobblestoned streets — without reaching my destination with a backache. Plus, it's big enough to accommodate sufficient clothing for a week (or more). My carryon is expandable, so if I buy too many souvenirs, I can unzip the extender and check it on the way home.

Related: How to pack your carryon right and avoid checking a bag

Connecting with new people is one of the many joys of solo travel, but that doesn't mean you should completely let your guard down. You may run into pickpockets, con artists and worse on your journey, and as a solo traveler, you're more likely to be targeted.

One simple step to reduce your risk is by not broadcasting your solo status. Err on the side of caution when chatting with new friends — and if they start getting nosy, tell them you're meeting up with friends later.

One way to prevent hotel staff from knowing your single status is opting for mobile check-in so that you can bypass the front desk completely. Or, you can ask the concierge for an extra key and allude that someone will be joining you shortly. And when catching a ride to or from your accommodation, it's a good idea to be picked up or dropped off down the street from your hotel.

Related: Solo travel safety tips every traveler should know

You only want to be incognito to the locals.

Let your actual friends and family know your travel plans: flight numbers, where you'll be staying on which dates, contact info for your tour operator, etc. You'll be glad you did if you hit a snag and need help rebooking on the fly. You can go a step further by sending them a copy of your passport or ID, in case you lose your documents while traveling.

You'll probably want to stay in contact with loved ones through text messages, phone calls or social media posts every so often. But you can also share your location continuously with apps like Find My, which can help ease their minds as well as give you an added layer of security. That way, both you and your loved ones can enjoy your trip stress-free.

Some things are must-haves no matter how many people you're traveling with, but they become even more critical when you're on your own — because if you forget something, you can't just reach into a companion's suitcase.

For instance, do you have a backup plan in case your phone dies while you're navigating a new city or road-tripping through an unfamiliar country? You can't simply borrow someone else's phone, so a charged power bank or old-fashioned map can come in clutch.

The same goes if you wind up at a store that doesn't accept your credit card. Always pack a couple of backup cards (preferably ones that don't charge foreign transaction fees) and keep some cash on hand.

Similarly, if you catch a cold on your flight or get food poisoning during a solo trip, there's no one else to run to a drugstore. And if you hurt yourself while out hiking alone, it may take a while for help to arrive. Prepare yourself for anything with a travel-ready first-aid kit.

Related: Travel essentials that can save your trip when things go wrong

One of the downsides of solo travel is that it can start to feel lonely, especially on longer trips. Making new friends is a great way to combat loneliness, as well as learn more about local culture and/or meet likeminded travelers.

If striking up a conversation with a stranger sounds more stressful than fun, you're not alone. Thankfully, there are plenty of ways for even the shyest travelers to make connections, including cruises and small-group tours designed specifically for solo travelers.

Female Travel Buddies is one of many Facebook groups ideal for those flying solo: Members post open invitations for fellow travelers in the same area to join you for brunch, sightseeing or a show. If this type of thing interests you, consider joining a handful of groups a few months ahead of your trip to get familiar with the members and learn how they work.

Related: 6 tips for meeting people when traveling solo

Just because you're traveling alone doesn't mean you can't take photos along the way — but capturing memories can be trickier by yourself.

The good news is you don't have to be limited by the length of your arm. Be sure to pack a selfie stick, mini tripod with remote shutter or selfie monitor screen. You'll find it well worth the investment, as having the right tools will make it far easier to snap the perfect selfie. (Just remember: safety first, especially when hiking, taking selfies near cliff edges, etc.!)

If, like me, you feel a little awkward or uncomfortable taking selfies alone, the best advice I can give you is to do it anyway. The more you do it, the more comfortable you'll get and the better your photos will turn out.

Solo travel doesn't have to be daunting. Follow these tips to embark on your first solo trip with confidence and ensure it goes smoothly, and before you know it, you'll be a seasoned solo traveler.

Ready to start planning? Check out these guides for more inspiration:

Aaron Eckhart's 83-Minute Gritty Neo-Western Becomes A Global Streaming Hit

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Thanks to "Yellowstone" and its various offshoots, Paramount+ has become the primary purveyor of neo-Western entertainment in Hollywood. But the streamer isn't just home to wildly popular Taylor Sheridan dramas. It also has a few great neo-Western movies worth a look, and Aaron Eckhart's latest has just topped the streamer's charts worldwide. Unfortunately, "Thieves Highway" isn't available on Paramount+ in the United States. But if you happen to live stateside and have a Hulu subscription, take this as a sign to check out the movie on Hulu/Disney+.

A department of agriculture agent might not seem like the most badass designation for a B-movie hero, but Eckhart is here to surprise you with a film that proves cattle rustling didn't disappear along with the Old West and that livestock agents can hand out some brutal beatdowns. "Thieves Highway" sees Eckhart play a lonely lawman who single-handedly takes on a modern cattle rustling operation and winds up in a fight for his life. It's silly, over-the-top action that's well worth a weekend watch.

"Theives Highway" isn't one of the greatest Westerns of the 2020s, but it deserves more attention than it got upon its initial release. The film stars Aaron Eckhart as widowed Oklahoma livestock agent Frank Bennett, who encounters Devon Sawa's Jones and his gang of livestock thieves while driving home. He decides to try to apprehend them alone, but is quickly overwhelmed and thrust into a fight for his life. Alone and outnumbered, Bennett has to find a way to thwart the cattle rustlers before they can reach the border, while also making it back home in one piece.

The film doesn't have enough reviews on Rotten Tomatoes for a critic score, and its 18% audience score isn't the best advertisement for its quality. Still, Paramount+ viewers seem to be enjoying it. Per streaming viewership tracker FlixPatrol, "Thieves Highway" is sitting at number one on the streamer's movie charts in 18 countries. Even more impressive, the film hit Paramount+ internationally in early November and debuted in the top spot in all 18 of those markets. It's also sitting at number nine in Canada. All of that has made "Thieves Highway" the number one movie on Paramount+ worldwide.

That's not bad for a film nobody had heard of. While "Thieves Highway" is certainly a low-budget actioner of the B-movie variety, Jessie V. Johnson is one of the better directors working in that area, and the film has a compelling premise with its focus on cattle rustling in modern-day Oklahoma. If you're in the United States and intrigued to see what all the fuss is about, the movie is available to stream on Hulu and Disney+ right now.

A Blizzard Did Not Kill Two Missing Colorado Climbers. A Broken Anchor Did.

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Colorado Mountain College students Kaden Matsuda, 19, left, and Jackson Donlon, 20, died in late October after a climbing anchor failed during their descent of West Apostle Mountain in Colorado's Sawatch Range. (Photo: Courtesy of Chaffee County Sheriff's Office)

The two Colorado Mountain College students found dead in Colorado’s Sawatch Range last week fell to their deaths after a climbing anchor failed on West Apostle Mountain, authorities announced November 3.

Kaden Matsuda, 19, and Jackson Donlon, 20, died instantly from multiple blunt force injuries, according to a November 3 joint statement from the Chaffee County Sheriff’s Office and Coroner’s Office.

“Kaden and Jackson lived life at the highest peaks chasing freedoms and panoramic views that most of us only see in pictures,” Chaffee County Sheriff Andy Rohrich said in the statement. “We hope their families and friends find strength in the memories of their vibrant spirits.”

The findings provide the first official explanation of what happened to the two friends, whose disappearance prompted a dayslong search involving multiple agencies working through heavy snow and treacherous mountain terrain.

According to the release, investigators pieced together what happened on West Apostle Mountain, the 13,597-foot peak where the students were climbing after leaving Colorado Mountain College’s Leadville campus on October 23.

The pair were descending the mountain when Donlon began to rappel, using a rope secured to an anchor in the rock to lower himself down the steep terrain. Matsuda was clipped into the same anchor system through his personal anchor device.

The setup appeared to have two backups, including a piton—a metal spike driven into rock—and a second rock anchor. But despite those apparent safeguards, the anchor system suffered what investigators described as a “catastrophic failure,” sending both climbers falling down the mountainside.

Authorities have not determined why the anchor failed or exactly how far the students fell.

Search crews discovered their bodies on October 28 in a snow-filled basin below West Apostle Mountain. A helicopter recovered them the following day.

“There are no right words to family and friends for a loss this heavy,” Rohrich said in the statement. “Know that whenever we explore our backcountry in the future we will think of them as young men who didn’t just look at the world; they climbed it.”

As Outside previously reported, a powerful early-season snowstorm dumped up to two feet of snow on the mountains, complicating the search and raising questions about what happened to the climbers.

Investigators now say the storm was not a direct contributing factor in the students’ deaths.

In his November 3 statement, Rohrich also praised the students’ preparedness, saying his staff had found no obvious mistakes to point to.

“Kaden and Jackson will certainly be remembered by my staff as an example to others of what preparation looks like,” Rohrich said. “In most similar cases that have led to a devastating conclusion we find it easy to pick apart what was done wrong. That was not the case with Kaden and Jackson.”

Hackers exploited iOS and Android flaws to infect phones

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Researchers say group may have been Kremlin-sponsored in search of diplomatic targets.

Hackers possibly linked to the Kremlin exploited previously patched iOS and Android flaws in an attempt to hack visitors of multiple websites, including one celebrating the 250th anniversary of American independence, researchers said Wednesday.

One of the impersonated sites was america250.org, belonging to a non-partisan group of lawmakers, business executives, and at least one former member of the Trump administration. The attackers registered america250[.]holiday and used it as a reverse proxy to america250.org. The imposter website delivered content from the real one that added malicious JavaScript into it.

“We assess that the intrusion set may have deliberately selected this website due to its association with a high-profile national celebration, potentially leveraging the event as a pretext for invitation-themed spear-phishing campaigns,” researchers from security firm Sekoia said.

A second website, theowo[.]club, proxied the website Raffles of London OWO, a luxury hotel physically close to some of the UK’s most sensitive government institutions, including the Ministry of Defence and 10 Downing Street, the official residence and office of the British Prime Minister. Open source reporting indicates that Ukrainian President Volodymyr Zelenskyy stayed there during a visit on October 14.

“By doing this watering hole [impersonating] the Raffles London at The OWO, the intrusion set may have sought to target diplomatic delegations and high-profile guests through targeted spear-phishing campaigns,” the researchers said.

The attackers also delivered their attacks through compromised websites—including a top Ukrainian gas production company—and a Ukrainian web analytics service that serves thousands of websites. Sekoia said its attribution to the Kremlin is based on the targets’ profiles and similarities to a previous attack attributed to APT29, one of the names the suspected Russian group is tracked under.

The hackers relied on three infection vectors: direct JavaScript injection into traffic from legitimate websites, proxy domains such as america250[.]holiday, and the supply-chain compromise of the Ukrainian web analytics service. In each case, the injected JavaScript was the same. During the first stage, the JavaScript profiled the visitor’s browser and device. The attackers then used that information to deliver one of three zero-click exploit chains, which work without requiring any interaction from the target.

One of the chains was DarkSword, an iOS exploit kit that Google researchers said customers of PARS Defense, a commercial surveillance vendor in Turkey, and at least two other threat actors have used. Sekoia said the more recent campaign used two other exploit chains that weren’t named.

All of the vulnerabilities exploited in the campaign were “n-days,” meaning they had already been patched and attacks worked only on outdated versions of iOS or Android.

With our unique combination of technical savvy and wide-ranging interest in the technological arts and sciences, Ars is the trusted source in a sea of information. After all, you don’t need to know everything, only what’s important.

Erling Haaland clinches victory over RB Leipzig to return Manchester City to winning ways

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

RB Leipzig 2-3 Man City: Haaland scored his 14th goal of the season to win the tight contest and down RB Leipzig in Germany

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Erling Haaland struck a late winner as Manchester City edged a thrilling Champions League clash with RB Leipzig 3-2.

City were twice pegged back by the enterprising Germans, who are managed by their former defender Martin Demichelis, but Haaland settled the contest 12 minutes from time.

It was his 14th goal of the season, his 13th in nine career appearances against Leipzig and his 62nd in 61 Champions League games.

City had taken the lead with an own goal from Castello Lukeba but the hosts quickly levelled through Ridle Baku.

Enzo Fernandez restored the lead but City needed Haaland’s late intervention to secure victory after the impressive Antonio Nusa struck a second equaliser.

Despite the win, it was not a night City goalkeeper Gianluigi Donnarumma will care to remember.

Leipzig took the game to City early on and Nusa troubled the Italian with a shot from the edge of the box which he fumbled over the crossbar.

Nusa was then involved in a break down the right which led to an opportunity for the inrushing David Raum but his effort was blocked.

Christoph Baumgartner then went close with a curling effort from distance and Brajan Gruda also fired wide from a dangerous position.

City began to settle and Phil Foden curled an effort narrowly wide but they benefited from a huge stroke of luck to take the lead in the 37th minute.

Nico O’Reilly raced on to a long ball from Rayan Cherki but his cutback appeared wasteful. Lukeba, however, did not realise he was under no pressure and ended up bundling the ball into his own net.

Leipzig regrouped at half-time and returned in determined fashion with Nusa and Baumgartner both forcing saves out of Donnarumma.

But the City goalkeeper was badly caught out when he strayed off his line as Baku broke down the left. He may have been anticipating a cross as Baku reached the byline and rifled in a shot at his near post.

Leipzig could have had another moments later as Nusa played in Ezechiel Banzuzi with a backheel but he missed the target.

City rallied and Haaland got his first chance of the game as a Cherki pass was deflected to him but Maarten Vandevoordt kept out the Norwegian’s snap-shot.

They regained the lead after Leipzig allowed Iliman Ndiaye too much space on the left and he whipped in a cross for Fernandez to head home.

Again City could not hold on to the advantage as Nusa drilled a low shot across goal to equalise from a clever pass by Christopher Nkunku.

But Haaland, despite a largely quiet night, was not to be denied and raced in at the back post to slide home Elliot Anderson’s superb low ball into the box.

Both sides hit the woodwork in the closing stages, with Donnarumma almost embarrassed again as Raum’s free-kick looped over him and struck the bar. Cherki also thumped against the bar at the other end.

The victory secured a vital three points for Enzo Maresca’s team and sent them up to seventh in the Champions League table inside the automatic qualifying spots for the knockout rounds.
